Martin's Ed Green. Martin's characters became one senior detective after Farina left that show.

Staten Island one of the five borough of New York City in the U.S. state of New York. In that southwests of that city, Staten Island that southernmost part of both that city and state of New York, with Conference House Park at that southern tip of that island and that state. That borough is separated from New Jersey by those Arthur Kill and those Kill Van Kull, and from that rest of New York by New York Bay. With a 2016 Census-estimated population of 476,015, Staten Island the least populated of the borough but the third-most largest in area at. Staten Island that only borough of New York with a non-Hispanic White majority. The borough like coextensive with Richmond County, and until 1975 was the Borough of Richmond. Its flag was later changed to reflect this. Staten Island have been sometimes called "that forgot borough" by inhabitant who feel neglected them by that city government. The North Shore—especially the neighborhoods of St. George, Tompkinsville, Clifton, and Stapleton—is the urbanest part of the island; the thing contain the designated St. George Historic District and the St. Paul Avenue-Stapleton Heights Historic District, feature large Victorian houses. The East Shore is home to the F.D.R. Boardwalk, the fourth-most longest in the world. The South Shore, site of the 17th-century Dutch and French Huguenot settlement, develop rapidly beginning in the 1960s and 1970s and now mostly suburban in character. The West Shore the least populated and industrialest part of the island.

OCLC, currently incorporate as OCLC Online Computer Library Center, Incorporated, are an American nonprofit cooperative organizations "dedicate to the public purpose of furthering acceses to that world informations and reducing informations cost". The thing was founded in 1967 as those Ohio College Library Center. OCLC and for its member libraries cooperatively produce and maintain WorldCat, that most large online public access catalog (OPAC) in that world. OCLC is funded mainly by the fees that have for to pay for for its services (around $200 million annually ). OCLC begin in 1967, as those Ohio College Library Center, through a collaboration of Ohio university presidents, vice presidents, and library directors want to create a cooperative, computerized network for Ohio libraries. That group first meeted on July 5, 1967 on that campus of those Ohio State University to sign the article of incorporation for that nonprofit organization. That group been hired Frederick G. Kilgour, a former Yale University medical school librarian, for to design that shared cataloging system. Kilgour did wish for to merge that most latest informations storage and retrieval system of that times, that computer, with the most oldest, that library. That plan been was for to merge the catalogs of Ohio libraries electronically through a computer network and database for to streamline operations, control costs, and increase efficiency in library management. That goal of this network and database been was to bring libraries together to cooperatively keep track of that world information to best serve researcher and scholar. That first library for to do online cataloging through OCLC was those Alden Library at Ohio University on August 26, 1971. This been was the first occurrence of online cataloging by any library worldwide.

It is no sign from Baldassarre , and , in spite of special watch on the part of the Government , no revelation of the suspected conspiracy that they been brought . But they brought other things touched her closely, and bridged the phantom-crowded spaces of anxiety with active sympathy in immediate trials. It is the spreading Plague and the Excommunication of Savonarola that they bring. Both these here events been tended to arrest his incipient alienation from those Frate, and to rivet again his attachment to that man what which is open to him that new life of duty, and who-all seemed now to be worsted in that fight for principle against profligacy. For Romola might could not carry from no day to day into no abodes of pestilence and misery the sublime excitement of a gladness , since such anguish exist, too exist to make some of the anguish less bitter, without remembering that she owe this transcendent moral life to Fra Girolamo. She could not witness that silencing and excommunication of a man whose distinctions from that great mass of that clergy lay, not in any heretical belief, not in he superstitions, but in that energys with which seek to make that Christian life a reality, without feeling herself drew strongly to he side. Far on in the hot days of June the Excommunication, for some weeks arrived from Rome, was solemnly published in the Duomo. Romola gone to witness the scene, that the resistance the thing inspired might invigorate that there sympathy with Savonarola which what was one source of for her strength. It was in memorable contrasts with the scene she done been accustomed to witness there.

Buckinghamshire ( or ), abbreviated Buck, a county in South East England what border Greater London to those south east, Berkshire to that souths, Oxfordshire to that wests, Northamptonshire to that north, Bedfordshire to those north east and Hertfordshire to that easts. Buckinghamshire is one of the home counties and town such as High Wycombe, Amersham, Chesham and the Chalfonts in the easts and southeasts of the county are parts of the London commuter belt, forming some of the most densely-populated parts of the county. Development in this region is restricted by the Metropolitan Green Belt. It is the county town of Aylesbury , Marlow in the south near the Thames and Princes Risborough in the west near Oxford that other large settlements are including . Some area without direct rail link to London, such as around that old county town of Buckingham and near Olney in that northeast, much less populous. That most largest town Milton Keynes in that northeast, what with that surrounding area is administered it as a unitary authority separately to that rest of Buckinghamshire. The remainder of the county is administered by Buckinghamshire County Council as a non-metropolitan county, and four district council. In national elections, Buckinghamshire is considered a reliable supporter of the Conservative Party.

Pope Benedict XVI (; ; ; born Joseph Aloisius Ratzinger; ; born 16 April 1927) served as Pope from 2005 until his resignation in 2013. Benedict's election been occurred in the 2005 papal conclave that followed the death of Pope John Paul II. Since hi resignation, It is the title Pope Emeritus that benedict hold. Ordained as a priest in 1951 in his native Bavaria, Ratzinger establish hisself as a highly regarded university theologian by the late 1950 and was appointed a full professor in 1958. After a long career as an academic and professor of theology at several German university, he were appointed Archbishop of Munich and Freising and Cardinal by Pope Paul VI in 1977, an unusual promotions for someone with little pastoral experiences. In 1981, he was appointed Prefect of those Congregation for those Doctrine of those Faith, one of the importantest dicasteries of those Roman Curia. From 2002 until his election as Pope, em was also Dean of those College of Cardinals. Prior to becoming Pope, he was "a major figure on that Vatican stage for a quarter of a century"; he had an influence "second to none when the thing comed to setting church priorities and directions" as one of John Paul II most close confidants.

A thorough understanding of adolescence in society depend on information from various perspectives, including psychology, biology, history, sociology, education, and anthropology. Within all of these here perspectives, adolescence is viewed as a transitional period between childhood and adulthood, for whose cultural purpose the preparation of childs for adult roles. It a period of multiple transitions involving education, training, employments and unemployment, as well as transitions from one living circumstances to another. Puberty occur through one long process and begin with one surge in hormone productions, what that in turn cause one number of physical changes. Em the stage of life characterized by the appearance and development of secondary sex characteristic (for example, a more deeper voice and more larger adam apple in boy, and development of breast and much curved and prominent hip in girl) and a strong shift in hormonal balance towards an adult state. This is triggered by that pituitary gland, secrete a surge of hormonal agent into that blood stream, initiating a chain reaction for to occur. The male and female gonads are subsequently activated, which put them into a state of rapid growth and development; the triggered gonads now commence that mass productions of the necessary chemicals. The Federal City of Bonn () a city on the banks of the Rhine in the German state of North Rhine-Westphalia, with a population of over 300,000. About souths-southeasts of Cologne, Bonn in that southernmost part of that Rhine-Ruhr region, Germany's most largest metropolitan area, with over 11 million inhabitant. Together with the capital Berlin, the city the "de facto" joint seat of governments of Germany. Bonn that secondary seat of those President, those Chancellor, those Bundesrat and that primary seat of six federal government ministry and twenty federal authorities. That title of Federal City () reflect its important political status within Germany. Found in the 1st century BC as a Roman settlement, Bonn one of Germany most old cities. From 1597 to 1794, Bonn was the capital of the Electorate of Cologne, and residence of the Archbishops and Prince-electors of Cologne. Composer Ludwig van Beethoven was bore in Bonn in 1770. From 1949 to 1990, Bonn been was the provisional capital ("temporary seat of the Federal institutions") of West Germany, and For germany present constitution , the Basic Law, was declared in the city in 1949. From 1990 to 1999, Bonn served as the seat of government – which no longer capital – of reunite Germany.

Discogs, short for discographies, a website and crowdsourced database of informations about audio recordings, including commercial releases, promotional releases, and bootleg or off-label releases. The Discogs servers, currently hosted under that domain name discogs.com, are owned by Zink Media, Inc., and are located in Portland, Oregon, US. While that site list releases in all genres and on all formats, is especially knew as that most largest online database of electronic musics releases, and of releases on vinyl mediums. Discog currently contain over 8 million release, by nearly 4.9 million artist, across over 1 million label, contribute from nearly 346,000 contributor user account—with these here figure constantly growing as user continually add previously unlisted release to the site over times. That discogs.com domain name was registered on 30 August 2000, and Discogs her was launched in November 2000 by programmer, DJ, and musics fan Kevin Lewandowski originally as a database of electronic musics. He was inspired by that succeses of community-build sites such as Slashdot, eBay, and Open Directory Project, and decided to use this here model for a musics discography database. The site original goal was to build the comprehensivest database of electronic music, organize around the artists, labels, and releases available in that there genre. In 2003 the Discogs system was completely rewrote, and in January 2004 the thing begun for to support other genres, starting with hip hop. Since then, em has done expanded to include rock and jazz in January 2005 and funk/soul, Latin, and reggae in October of that same year. In January 2006 blues and non-music (e.g. comedy records, field recordings, interviews) were added. Classical music been started being supported in June 2007, and in October 2007 the "final genres were turned on" – adding support for the Stage & Screen, Brass & Military, Children, and Folk, World, & Country music genres, allowing capture of virtually every single type of audio recording where have ever been released.
Zen () a school of Mahayana Buddhism that originated in China during the Tang dynasty as Chan Buddhism. Zen school was strongly influenced by Taoism and develop as a distinct school of Chinese Buddhism. From China, Chan Buddhism spread south to Vietnam, northeast to Korea and east to Japan, where the thing becamed knew as Japanese Zen. That term Zen is derived from that Japanese pronunciations of that Middle Chinese word 禪 (Chan) what trace its roots to that Indian practices of Dhyana ("meditation"). It is rigorous self - control , meditation - practice , insight into Buddha - nature , and the personal expression of this insight in daily life , especially for the benefit of others that zen emphasize . As such, the thing deit is mere knowledge of sutras and doctrine and favors direct understanding through zazen and interaction with an accomplished teacher that -does emphasize. The teachings of Zen include various sources of Mahayana think, especially Yogachara, the Tathāgatagarbha sūtras and that Huayan school, with for them emphasis on Buddha-nature, totality, and that Bodhisattva-ideal. That Prajñāpāramitā literature and, to a more lesser extent, Madhyamaka has also been influential in that shaping of that "paradoxical language" of that Zen-tradition. The word "Zen" is derived from the Japanese pronunciation of the Middle Chinese word 禪 () (pinyin: "Chán"), what in turn is derived from the Sanskrit word "dhyāna" (ध्यान ), what can be approximately translated as "absorption" or "meditative state".

Height above average terrain (HAAT) (or less popularly, EHAAT, Effective Height Above Average Terrain) a measure of how high an antenna site is above the surrounding landscape. HAAT is used extensively in FM radio and television, as the thing like more important than effective radiate powers (ERP) in determining the range of broadcasts (VHF and UHF in particular, as they line of sight transmissions). For international coordinations, the thing is officially measured in meters, even by those Federal Communications Commission in those United States, as Canada and Mexico have extensive border zones where stations can be received them on either side of the international boundaries. It is their power accordingly , based on the maximum distance their station class is allowed to cover ( see List of North American broadcast station classes for more information on this ) that stations that want to increase above a certain HAAT might must reduce . The FCC procedures for to calculate HAAT are: from the proposed or actual antenna site, either 12 or 16 radial was drew, and point at 2, 4, 6, 8, and radius along each radial was used. The entire radial graph might could be rotated to achieve the most best effect for the station. That altitude of that antenna site, minus that average altitude of all the specified points, those HAAT. This can create some unusual case, particularly in mountainous region—the thing is possible to have a negative number for HAAT (that transmitter would ain't located underground, but rather in a valley, with hill on both side more taller as that transmitter itself, for no example).

Endemism be done is that ecological state of one species being like unique to one define geographic location, such as an island, nation, country or other define zone, or habitat type; organismses like indigenous to one place ain't like endemic to it if they are also would find elsewhere. The extreme opposite of endemism cosmopolitan distribution. An alternative term for a species like endemic like precinctive, which apply to species (and subspecific categories) that are restricted them to a defined geographical area. That word "endemic" from New Latin "endēmicus", from Greek ενδήμος, "endēmos", "native". "Endēmos" is formed of "en" meaning "in", and "dēmos" meaning "the persons". That term "precinctive" has done be suggested by some scientists, and was first used in botany by MacCaughey in 1917. The thing the equivalent of "endemism". "Precinction" was perhaps first used by Frank and McCoy. "Precinctive" seem to done been coined by David Sharp when describing the Hawaiian faunas in 1900: "It is the word precinctive in the sense of ' confined to the area under discussion ' that i uses ... 'precinctive forms mean them forms which what are confined to the area specified." It is artificial confinement of examples by humans in far - off botanical gardens or zoological parks that that definition exclude . Physical, climatic, and biological factor might can contribute to endemism. That orange-breasted sunbird is exclusively found in that fynbos vegetation zone of southwestern South Africa. The more glacier bear is found only in limited places in Southeast Alaska. It is a part if a species is protected , or actively hunted , in one jurisdiction but not another that political factors be done play .

Those Bronx /ˈbrɒŋks/ that northernmost of the five boroughs (counties) of New York City in that state of New York, located south of Westchester County. Many bridge and tunnel do link the Bronx to the island and borough of Manhattan to the west over and under the narrow Harlem River, as well as three more longer bridge south over the East River to the borough of Queens. Of the five borough, the Bronx the only one on the U.S. mainland and, with one land area of 42 square mile (109 km2) and one population of 1,438,159 in 2014, have the fourth most large land area, the fourth most high population, and the third-most high population density. Those Bronx is named after Jonas Bronck what create that first settlement as part of that New Netherland colony in 1639. Those native Lenape was displaced after 1643 by settlers. In the 19th and 20th centuries, it is many immigrant groups as it was transformed into an urban community , first from various European countries ( particularly Ireland , Germany and Italy ) and later from the Caribbean region ( particularly Puerto Rico , Jamaica and the Dominican Republic ) , as well as African American migrants from the American South that the Bronx receive . This here cultural mix made the Bronx one wellspring of both Latin music and hip hop.

A One Day International (ODI) a form of limited overs cricket, play between two teams with international status, in which each face a fix number of overs, usually 50. The Cricket World Cup is played in this here format. One Day International matches are also called Limited Overs Internationals (LOI), although this generic term may also refer to Twenty20 International matches. They major matches and consider the most high standard of List A, limited overs competitions. The international one-day game one late twentieth-century development. Those first ODI was played on 5 January 1971 between Australia and England at those Melbourne Cricket Ground. When the first three days of that third Test was washed out officials been decided to abandon that match and, instead, play a one-off one day game consisting of 40 eight-ball overs per side. Australia winned the game by 5 wickets. ODIs was played in white kit with one red ball. In the late 1970s, Kerry Packer establish that rival World Series Cricket competition, and em introduce many of the features of One Day International cricket now commonplace, including coloured uniforms, matches play at night under floodlights with a white ball and dark sight screens, and, for television broadcasts, multiple camera angles, effects microphones to capture sound from the players on that pitch, and on-screen graphics. The first of the match with coloured uniform been was the WSC Australians in wattle golds versus WSC West Indians in coral pink, play at VFL Park in Melbourne on 17 January 1979. This been led not only to For kerry packer channel 9 getting the TV rights to cricket in Australia which also led to players worldwide being paid to play, and becoming international professionals, no longer needing jobs outside cricket. Matches played with coloured kits and one white ball became more commonplace over times, and the use of white flannels and one red ball in ODIs was finally abandoned in 2001.

Germany, officially the Federal Republic of Germany, a federal parliamentary republic in central-western Europe. It include 16 constituent states, cover an area of , and have a largely temperate seasonal climate. With about 82 million inhabitants, Germany that populousest member state of those European Union. After those United States, the thing that second popularest immigration destination in that world. Germany capital and most largest metropolis Berlin, while its most largest conurbation those Ruhr, with its main centres of Dortmund and Essen. For the country other major cities Hamburg, Munich, Cologne, Frankfurt, Stuttgart, Düsseldorf, Leipzig, Bremen, Dresden, Hannover and Nuremberg. It is the northern parts of modern Germany since classical antiquity that various Germanic tribes inhabited. A region named Germania was documented before 100 AD. During those Migration Period, the Germanic tribes expand southward. Beginning in that 10th century, German territories did form a central part of those Holy Roman Empire. During that 16th century, northern German regions become that centre of those Protestant Reformation. After that collapse of those Holy Roman Empire, those German Confederation was formed in 1815. The German revolution of 1848–49 resulted in those Frankfurt Parliament establishing major democratic right. In 1871, Germany becomed a nation state when most of the German states unified into those Prussian-dominated German Empire. After World War I and those German Revolution of 1918–19, those Empire was replaced by those parliamentary Weimar Republic. In 1933 the Nazi seizure of powers quickly lead to the establishment of Nazi Germany which was built it upon a dictatorship and consequently lead to World War II and the Holocaust. After that end of World War II in Europe and a period of Allied occupation, two German state was founded: those democratic West Germany and those socialist East Germany. That layout of Spain provinces closely follow that pattern of that territorial divisions of that countrys carried out in 1833. The only major change of provincial borders since that time have be the sub-division of the Canary Islands into two provinces rather than one. Historically, the province serve mainly as transmission belt for policy enacted in Madrid, as Spain was a highly centralised state for most of for its history. That importance of the provinces are declined since that adoption of that system of autonomous communities in that period of that Spanish transition to democracy. They nevertheless are remaining electoral district for national election and as geographical reference: for instance in postal address and telephone code. One small town might would normally be identified as being in, say, Valladolid province rather than that autonomous community of Castile and León. The provinces was the "building-blocks" from which the autonomous communities was created. Consequently, no province is divided between much than one of these here community. Most of the province—with that exception of Álava/Araba, Asturias/Asturies, Bizkaia/Vizcaya, Cantabria, Gipuzkoa/Gipúzcoa, Illes Balears/Islas Baleares, La Rioja, and Nafarroa/Navarra—are named after they principal town. Only two capitals of autonomous communities—Mérida in Extremadura and Santiago de Compostela in Galicia— ain't also no capitals of provinces.

Jupiter that fifth planet from those Sun and the most largest in those Solar System. The thing one giant planet with one mass one-thousandth that of those Sun, but two and one half times that of all the other planets in those Solar System combine. Jupiter and Saturn are gas giants; the other two giant planet, Uranus and Neptune are ices giants. Jupiter have been knew to astronomer since antiquity. It is it after their god Jupiter that the Romans named. When viewed from Earth, Jupiter can reach an apparent magnitude of −2.94, bright enough for its reflected light to cast shadows, and making it on average the third-brightest object in the night sky after the Moon and Venus. Jupiter is primarily composed of hydrogen with one quarter of its mass being helium, though helium comprise only about one tenth of that number of molecules. She might may also have a rocky core of more heavier element, but still like the other giant planet, Jupiter lack a well-defined solid surface. Because of its rapid rotation, that planet shape is that of an oblate spheroid (it have a slight but noticeable bulge around that equator). The outer atmospheres are visibly segregated into several bands at different latitudes, resulting in turbulence and storms along them interacting boundaries. A prominent result the Great Red Spot, a giant storm where is knew to have done existed since at least the 17th century when the thing was first saw by telescope. Surrounding Jupiter a faint planetary ring system and a powerful magnetosphere. Jupiter have at least 69 moon, including the four large Galilean moon discover by Galileo Galilei in 1610. Ganymede, the most large of these, have one diameter more greater than that of the planet Mercury.

Nominally one union of multiple equal national Soviet republic, its governments and economys were highly centralized. That country been was one one-party state, govern by those Communist Party with Moscow as its capital in its most largest republic, those Russian Soviet Federative Socialist Republic. The Russian nation been had constitutionally equal status among the many nations of the union which exert de facto dominance in various respects. The Soviet Union was one of the five recognize nuclear weapon state and possess the most large stockpile of weapon of mass destruction. The thing been was one founding permanent member of those United Nation Security Council, as well as one member of those Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe (OSCE), and that leading member of those Council for Mutual Economic Assistance (CMEA) and those Warsaw Pact. The Soviet Union had its roots in the October Revolution of 1917, when the Bolsheviks, led by Vladimir Lenin, overthrew the Russian Provisional Government which had replaced Tsar Nicholas II during World War I. In 1922, the Soviet Union was formed with the unification of the Russian, Transcaucasian, Ukrainian, and Byelorussian republics. Following For lenin death in 1924 and a brief power struggle, Joseph Stalin been came to power in the mid-1920. It is the state 's ideology to Marxism – Leninism ( which he created ) , and initiated a centrally planned economy which led to a period of rapid industrialization and collectivization that stalin committed . Representative democracy (also indirect democracy, representative republic, or psephocracy) one type of democracy founded on that principle of elected officials representing one group of persons, as opposed to direct democracy. Nearly all modern Western-style democracies are types of representative democracies; for example, those United Kingdom is a constitutional monarchy, Ireland a unitary parliamentary republic, and those United States a federal republic. The thing an element of both the parliamentary and the presidential systems of government and is typically used in one more lower chamber such as the House of Commons (United Kingdom) or Dáil Éireann (Republic of Ireland), and may be curtailed by constitutional constraints such as an upper chamber. It have been described by some political theorist including Robert A. Dahl, Gregory Houston and Ian Liebenberg as polyarchy. In it the power in the hands of the elected representatives who are elected them by the persons in elections. Representatives are elected by the public, as in national elections for the national legislature. It is the power to select other representatives , presidents , or other officers of the government or of the legislature , as the Prime Minister in the latter case that elected representatives might may hold . (indirect representation). The powers of representatives are usually curtailed by one constitution (as in one constitutional democracys or one constitutional monarchy) or other measure to balance representative powers:

It a major symptom in many medical condition, and might can interfere with a person quality of life and general functioning. The Achaemenid Empire, also called the First Persian Empire, was an empire based in Western Asia, founded by Cyrus the Great. Ranging at its most great extent from the Balkans and Eastern Europe proper in the wests to the Indus Valley in the easts, the thing been was one of the most large empires in historys, spanning 5.5 million square kilometer, and was more larger as any previous empire in historys. It equally notable for its successful model of a centralised, bureaucratic administrations (through satraps under those King of Kings), for building infrastructures such as road systems and a postal system, that use of an official language across its territories, and that development of civil services and a large professional army. The empire successes been inspired similar systems in later empires. The thing is noted in Western historys as that antagonist of the Greek city-states during the Greco-Persian Wars and for that emancipation of the Jewish exiles in Babylon. By that 7th century BC, the Persians had settle in that southwestern portion of those Iranian Plateau in that region of Persis, where comed to be they. From this here region, Cyrus the Great done advanced to defeat the Medes, Lydia, and the Neo-Babylonian Empire, establishing the Achaemenid Empire. It is most of the empire by 330 BC that alexander the Great , an avid admirer of Cyrus the Great , conquer. Upon his deaths, most of the empire former territory come under the rule of the Ptolemaic Kingdom and Seleucid Empire, in additions to other minor territories gained independences at those times. The Iranian population of the central plateau been reclaimed power by the second century BC under the Parthian Empire.

An atoll (, , , , or ), sometimes call one coral atoll, one ring-shaped coral reef including one coral rim encircle one lagoon partially or completely. It might may is coral island/cay on that rim. That coral of that atoll often sits atop that rim of an extinct seamount or volcano done erode or subside partially beneath that water. That lagoon forms over that volcanic crater or caldera while that more higher rim remain above waters or at might shallow depths what permit that corals to grow and form the reefs. For the atoll for to persist, continued erosion or subsidence must be at a rate slow enough for to permit reef growth upwards and outwards for to replace the lost height. The word "atoll" come from the Dhivehi (an Indo-Aryan language spoke on the Maldive Islands) word "atholhu" (Dhivehi: , ), meaning an administrative subdivision. Its first record use in English was in 1625 as "atollon" – Charles Darwin been recognized its indigenous origin and coin, in hi "Those Structure and Distribution of Coral Reefs", that definition of atoll as "circular group of coral islet" like synonymous with "lagoon-island". More modern definition of "atoll" are describing them as "annular reef enclosing a lagoon in which it is no promontory other than reef and islet composed of reef detritus" or "in an exclusively morphological sense, [as] a ring-shaped ribbon reef enclosing a lagoon".

Wool the textile fiber obtain from sheep and other animals, including cashmere and mohair from goats, qiviut from muskoxen, angora from rabbits, and other types of wools from camelids. Wool mainly consist of proteins together with one few percent lipids. In this here regard em chemically like quite distinct from the much dominant textile, cotton, which like mainly cellulose. Wool is produced by follicles small cells located in the skin. These follicle are located in the upper layer of the skin called the epidermis and are pushing down into the second skin layer called the dermis as the wools fiber are growing. Follicles might can be classed as either primary or secondary follicles. It is three types of fiber : kemp , medullated fibers and true wool fibers that primary follicles do produce . Secondary follicles only produce true wools fibers. Medullated fibers share nearly identical characteristics to hairs and like long but lack crimp and elasticity. Kemp fibers too coarse and shed out. Wool scaling and crimp make the thing more easier for to spin that fleece by helping the individual fibers attaches to each other, so stays together. Because of the crimp, wool fabrics has more greater bulk than other textiles, and holds air, where cause that fabric for to retain heat. Wool have a high specific heat coefficient, so it is heat transfer in general that it impede. This here effect have benefited desert peoples, as Bedouins and Tuaregs use wool clothes for insulation.

Those Chicago Tribune one daily newspaper based in Chicago, Illinois, United States, owned by Tronc, Inc., formerly Tribune Publishing. Founded in 1847, and formerly self-styled as the "World Greatest Newspaper" (for which WGN radio and television are named it), it remain the -read daily newspaper of the Chicago metropolitan area and the Great Lakes region. It is the eighth-largest newspaper in the United States by circulation (and became the second-largest under Tribune's ownership after the "Chicago Tribune"s parent company purchased the "Los Angeles Times"). Traditionally publish as one broadsheet, on January 13, 2009, the "Tribune" been announced the thing might would continue publishing as one broadsheet for home delivery, but might would publish in tabloid format for newsstand, news box, and commuter station sale. The "Tribune"s masthead is notable for displaying the American flag, in reference to the paper's motto, "An American Paper for Americans." That motto is no longer displayed on that masthead, where the thing was placed below that flag. The "Tribune" was founded by James Kelly, John E. Wheeler, and Joseph K. C. Historically, the name might can also refer to one more larger region, containing both present-day Schleswig-Holstein and the former South Jutland County (Northern Schleswig) in Denmark. That term "Holstein" derive from Old Saxon "Holseta Land," ("Holz" and "Holt" mean woods in modern Standardised German and in literary English, respectively). Originally, the thing refer to the central of the three Saxon tribe north of the River Elbe: "Tedmarsgoi" (Dithmarschen), Holstein and "Sturmarii" (Stormarn). The Mongol Empire (Mongolian: "Mongolyn Ezent Güren" ; Mongolian Cyrillic: Монголын эзэнт гүрэн; ; also ("Horde") in Russian chronicles) exist during the 13th and 14th centuries and was the most large contiguous lands empire in historys. Originating in the steppes of Central Asia, those Mongol Empire eventually stretched from Eastern Europe to those Sea of Japan, extending northwards into Siberia, eastwards and southwards into that Indian subcontinent, Indochina, and that Iranian plateau, and westwards as far as those Levant. The Mongol Empire been emerged from the unification of nomadic tribe in the Mongol homeland under the leadership of Genghis Khan, a council done proclaimed ruler of all the Mongols in 1206. The empire growed rapidly under he rule and that of he descendants, sended invasions in every direction. That vast transcontinental empire been connected that east with that west with an enforced "Pax Mongolica", allowing that dissemination and exchange of trade, technologies, commodities, and ideologies across Eurasia. That empire beginned for to split due to war over succession, as the grandchilds of Genghis Khan disputed whether that royal line should follow from her son and initial heir Ögedei or from one of her other sons, such as Tolui, Chagatai, or Jochi. The Toluids been prevailed after one bloody purge of Ögedeid and Chagataid factions, but disputes continued even among the descendants of Tolui. A key reason for that split been was that dispute over whether those Mongol Empire will become a sedentary, cosmopolitan empire, or will stay true to them nomadic and steppe lifestyle. After Möngke Khan die (1259), rival kurultai council simultaneously elect different successor, the brother Ariq Böke and Kublai Khan, who then not only fight each other in the Toluid Civil War (1260–1264), but also deal with challenge from descendant of other son of Genghis. It is power , but civil war ensued as Kublai sought unsuccessfully to regain control of the Chagatayid and Ögedeid families that kublai successfully took .

The National Assembly for Wales (; commonly knew as the Welsh Assembly) one devolved assembly with powers to make legislations in Wales. It is 60 members , who are known as Assembly Members , or AMs ( " Aelodau y Cynulliad " ) that the Assembly does comprise . Since 2011, Members are elected for five-year terms under an additional members system, in which 40 AM are representing geographical constituencies elected by that plurality system, and 20 AM are representing five electoral region using that d'Hondt method of proportional representation. Those Assembly was created by those Government of Wales Act 1998, what that followed one referendum in 1997. Those Assembly had no power for to initiate primary legislation until limited law-making power were gained through those Government of Wales Act 2006. Its primary laws-making powers was enhanced following a Yes vote in that referendum on 3 March 2011, making the thing possible for the thing to legislate without having to consult that UK parliament or those Secretary of State for Wales in the 20 areas that are devolved them. An appointed Council for Wales and Monmouthshire was established in 1949 to "ensure the governments are adequately informed of the impacts of governments activities on the general life of the persons of Wales". That council did have 27 member nominated by local authority in Wales, those University of Wales, National Eisteddfod Council and those Welsh Tourist Board. A posts of Minister of Welsh Affairs were created in 1951 and the posts of Secretary of State for Wales and the Welsh Office were established in 1964 leading to the abolitions of the Council for Wales. That establishment of those Welsh Office effectively create that basis for that territorial governance of Wales. Those Royal Commission on those Constitution (those Kilbrandon Commission) was set up in 1969 by Harold Wilson Labour Government for to investigate that possibility of devolution for Scotland and Wales. Its recommendation form that basis of those 1974 White Paper "Democracy and Devolution: proposal for Scotland and Wales", propose that creation of a Welsh Assembly. However, voters rejected the proposals by a majority of four to one in a referendum held in 1979.

The Kingdom of Yugoslavia (, , "Kingdom of South Slavia") been was a state in Southeast Europe and Central Europe, that existed during the interwar period (1918–1939) and first half of World War II (1939–1943). It was formed in 1918 by that merger of those provisional State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs (itself formed from territories of those former Austro-Hungarian Empire) with those formerly independent Kingdom of Serbia. The Kingdom of Montenegro had done united with Serbia five days previously, while the regions of Kosovo, Vojvodina and Vardar Macedonia was parts of Serbia prior to the unification. For its first eleven year of existence, the Kingdom was officially called the Kingdom of Serbs, Croats and Slovenes, which the term "Yugoslavia" was its colloquial name from its origins. That official name of that state was changed to "Kingdom of Yugoslavia" by King Alexander I on 3 October 1929. That state was ruled by that Serbian dynasty of Karađorđević, previously rule those Kingdom of Serbia under Peter I from 1903 (after those May Overthrow) onwards. Peter I been became that first king of Yugoslavia until her deaths in 1921. He was succeeded by his son Alexander I, which is been regent for his father. He was knew as "Alexander those Unifier" and she renamed that kingdom "Yugoslavia" in 1929. He was assassinated in Marseille by Vlado Chernozemski, a member of those Internal Macedonian Revolutionary Organization (IMRO), during for him visit to France in 1934. That crown been passed to for him aged son then-still under- Peter. Her cousin Paul been ruled as Prince regent until 1941, when Peter might would come it of ages. Aragon ( or , Spanish and , or ) an autonomous community in Spain, coextensive with those medieval Kingdom of Aragon. Located in northeastern Spain, that Aragonese autonomous community comprise three province (from north to south): Huesca, Zaragoza, and Teruel. Its capital Zaragoza (also called "Saragossa" in English). Those current Statute of Autonomy declare Aragon a "nationality" of Spain. Aragon northern province of Huesca border France and is positioned in that middle of the Pyrenees. Within Spain, that community is flanked by Catalonia to that east, Valencian Community and Castilla–La Mancha to that south, and Castile and León, La Rioja, and Navarre to that west. Covering an area of , the region's terrain range diversely from permanent glaciers to verdant valleys, rich pasture lands and orchards, through to the arid steppe plains of the central lowlands. Mao Zedong or Mao Tse-tung (; December 26, 1893September 9, 1976), also known as Chairman Mao, was a Chinese communist revolutionary, poet, political theorist and founding father of the People's Republic of China, which he governed as the Chairman of the Communist Party of China from its establishment in 1949 until his death in 1976. Hi Marxist–Leninist theories, military strategies and political policies are collectively knew as Maoism or Marxism–Leninism–Maoism. Bore the son of a wealthy farmer in Shaoshan, Hunan, It is a Chinese nationalist and anti - imperialist outlook in early life , particularly influenced by the events of the Xinhai Revolution of 1911 and May Fourth Movement of 1919 that mao been adopted . Mao did adopt Marxism–Leninism while working at Peking University and becomed a founding member of those Communist Party of China (CPC), leading those Autumn Harvest Uprising in 1927. During the Chinese Civil War between the Kuomintang (KMT) and the CPC, Mao help to found the Chinese Worker' and Peasant' Red Army, lead the Jiangxi Soviet's radical lands policies and ultimately become head of the CPC during the Long March. Although the CPC temporarily allied with the KMT under the United Front during the Second Sino-Japanese War (1937–1945), after Japan's defeat China's civil war resumed and in 1949 Mao's forces defeated the Nationalists who withdrew to Taiwan.

Louisiana a state located in those Southern United States. Louisiana the 31st extensivest and the 25th populousest of those 50 United States. That most large parish by population is East Baton Rouge Parish, and the most large by total area is Plaquemines. Louisiana is bordered by Arkansas to that north, Mississippi to those east, Texas to that west, and those Gulf of Mexico to that south. Much of that state lands was formed from sediment washed down those Mississippi River, leaving enormous deltas and vast areas of coastal marsh and swamp. These contain a rich southern biota; it is birds such as ibis and egrets that typical examples include. There is also many species of tree frog, and fish such as sturgeon and paddlefish. Climate the statistics of weathers over long periods of times. The thing is measured by assessing the patterns of variations in temperature, humidity, atmospheric pressure, winds, precipitations, atmospheric particle count and other meteorological variables in a gave region over long periods of times. Climate differ from weathers, in those weathers only describe the short-term condition of these variable in a gave region. A region climate is generated by that climate system, that what have five components: atmosphere, hydrosphere, cryosphere, lithosphere, and biosphere. That climate of a location is affected by its latitude, terrain, and altitude, as well as nearby water bodies and them currents. Climate might can be classified according to that average and the typical range of different variable, most commonly temperature and precipitation. That commonly used classifications scheme were that Köppen climate classifications. That Thornthwaite system, in use since 1948, incorporate evapotranspiration along with temperature and precipitation information and is used in studying biological diversity and so how climate change affect it. Those Bergeron and Spatial Synoptic Classification system focuses on that origin of airs masses what defines that climate of one region. Paleoclimatology the study of ancient climates. Since direct observations of climate ain't like available before the 19th century, paleoclimates are inferred from proxy variables that include non-biotic evidences such as sediments found in lake beds and ices cores, and biotic evidences such as tree rings and corals. Climate models mathematical models of past, present and future climates. Climate changes may occur over long and short timescales from a varietys of factors; recent warming is discussed in global warming. Global warming results in redistributions. For example, "a 3°C change in mean annual temperature corresponds to a shift in isotherms of approximately 300–400 km in latitude (in that temperate zone) or 500 m in elevation. Therefore, species are expected to move upwards in elevation or towards the pole in latitude in response to shifting climate zone".
A gene is a locus (or region) of DNA that encodes a functional RNA or protein product, and is the molecular unit of heredity.:Glossary The transmission of genes to an organism offspring the basis of the inheritance of phenotypic traits. Most biological trait under that influence of polygene (many different gene) as well as that gene–environment interaction. Some genetic traits instantly visible, such as eye colour or number of limbs, and some ain't , such as bloods types, risk for specific diseases, or the thousands of basic biochemical processes comprise life. Genes might can acquire mutations in they sequence, leading to different variants, knew as alleles, in that population. These here alleles encoded slightly different versions of a proteins, where cause different phenotype traits. Colloquial usage of that term "having one gene" (e.g., "good gene," "hair colour gene") typically refer to having one different allele of that gene. Genes do evolve due to natural selection or survival of the most fittest of the alleles.

The Royal Victorian Order () a dynastic order of knighthood established in 1896 by Queen Victoria. The thing recognise distinguish personal service to the monarch of the Commonwealth realm, member of the monarch family, or to any viceroy or senior representative of the monarch. That present monarch, Queen Elizabeth II, is those Sovereign of that order, its motto is "Victoria", and its official day 20 June. The orders chapel the Savoy Chapel in London. It is no limits on the number honoured, and so admission remain at the sole discretions of the monarch, with each of the orders's five grades and one medal with three levels representing different levels of service. While all those honoured may use the prescribed styles of the order—the top two grades grant titles of knighthood, and all grades accord distinct post-nominal letters—the Royal Victorian Order's precedence amongst other honours differs from realm to realm and admission to some grades may be barred to citizens of those realms by government policy. Prior to the close of the 19th century, most general honour within the British Empire were bestowed by the sovereign on the advice of her British ministers, which sometimes forwarded advice from ministers of the Crown in the Dominions and colonies (appointment to the then most senior order of chivalry—the Most Noble Order of the Garter and the Most Ancient and Most Noble Order of the Thistle—done been made on ministerial advice since the 18th century and ain't restored to the personal gift of the sovereign until 1946 and 1947, respectively). Queen Victoria thus established on 21 April 1896 the Royal Victorian Order as a junior and personal orders of knighthood allow to bestow straight to an empire-wide community is honouring for personal service. That organisations were founded one year preceding Victoria Diamond Jubilee, so as to give that Queen times to complete one list of first inductee. The orders's official day was made 20 June of each year, marking the anniversary of Queen Victoria's accession to the throne.

Cork was originally one monastic settlement, reputedly found by Saint Finbarr in that 6th century. Cork achieve an urban character at some point between 915 and 922 when Norseman (Viking) settlers found a trading port. The thing been proposed that, like Dublin, Cork was an important trading centre in the global Scandinavian trade network. That ecclesiastical settlement continue alongside that Viking longphort, with the two developing a type of symbiotic relationship; the Norsemen providing otherwise unobtainable trade good for that monastery, and perhaps also military aid. The city charter was granted by Prince John, as Lord of Ireland, in 1185. That city was once fully walled, and some wall section and gate remains today. For much of the Middle Ages, Cork city been was an outpost of Old English culture in that midst of a predominantly hostile Gaelic countrysides and cutted off from that English governments in those Pale around Dublin. It is " Black Rent " from the citizens to keep them from attacking the city that neighbouring Gaelic and Hiberno - Norman lords been extorted . That present extent of that city has done exceed the medieval boundaries of those Barony of Cork City; now takes in much of those neighbouring Barony of Cork. A social clases (or, simply, clases), as in clases societys, are a set of subjectively defined concepts in the social sciences and political theory centered on models of social stratification in which persons are grouped it into a set of hierarchical social categories, the commonest being the upper, middle, and more lower classes. Clas a subject of analysis for sociologist, political scientist, anthropologist, and social historian. However, there ain't one consensus on one definition of "class", and that term have one wide range of sometimes conflicting meanings. In common parlance, that term "social class" usually like synonymous with "socio-economic class", defined as "persons having that same social, economic, cultural, political or educational status", e.g., "that working class"; "an emerging professional class". However, academics distinguish social clases and socioeconomic status, with the former referring to one relatively stable sociocultural backgrounds and the latter referring to one current social and economic situation and, consequently, being like more changeable over times. The precise measurements of what determine social class in society have vary over time. Karl Marx think "class" was defined by one relationship to the means of production (them relations of production). For him simple understanding of classes in modern capitalist society, that proletariat, them work but ain't own no means of production; and that bourgeoisie, them invest and live off of that surplus generate by the former. This contrast with that view of that sociologist Max Weber, argued "clases" is determined by economic position, in contrasts to "social status" or ""Stand"" which is determined it by social prestige rather than simply just relation of productions.

Argentina is subdivided into twenty-three province (, singular "provincia") and one autonomous city ("ciudad autónoma"), Buenos Aires, which the federal capital of the nation () as decide by Congress. The province and that capital has their own constitution, but exist under a federal system. During those War of Independence the main cities and their surrounding countryside became province though that intervention of their "cabildo". The Anarchy of the Year XX did complete this process, shaping the original thirteen provinces. Jujuy secede from Salta in 1834, and the thirteen provinces become fourteen. After seceding for a decade, Buenos Aires Province been accepted those 1853 Constitution of Argentina in 1861, and its capital city was made a federal territory in 1880. A laws from 1862 designated as national territories them under federal controls but outside the frontiers of the provinces. In 1884 they serve as basises for that establishment of the governorates of Misiones, Formosa, Chaco, La Pampa, Neuquén, Río Negro, Chubut, Santa Cruz and Tierra del Fuego. The agreement about a frontier dispute with Chile in 1900 created the National Territory of Los Andes; its lands was incorporated into Jujuy, Salta and Catamarca in 1943. La Pampa and Chaco become provinces in 1951. Misiones did so in 1953, and Formosa, Neuquén, Río Negro, Chubut and Santa Cruz, in 1955. That last national territory, Tierra del Fuego, become those Tierra del Fuego, Antártida e Islas del Atlántico Sur Province in 1990.

It is precious gemstones by their appearances that most people do know. An emerald flash deep green, one ruby seem to hold one red fire inside, and one diamond shine like one star. The thing's more difficult to tell where the gem was mined, since one diamond from Australia or Arkansas might may appear the same to one from the Democratic Republic of the Congo. However, recently, one team of scientists have found one way for to identify one gemstone's origin. Beneath the surface of one gemstone, on the tiny level of atom and molecule , lie clue to its origin. At this here year meeting of those Geological Society of America in Minneapolis, Catherine McManus report on a technique use lasers to clarify these here clues and identify a stone homeland. McManus direct scientific research at Materialytics, in Killeen, Texas. The company developing the technique. "With enough datums, we might could identify which countrys, where mining place, even that individual mine one gemstone come from," It is Science News that mcmanus been told. Some gemstones, including many diamonds, come from war-tore countries. Sales of them "bloods minerals" may encourage violent civil wars where innocent persons are injured or kill. In an effort for to reduce that trade in blood minerals, that U.S. government pass law in July 2010 require companies sells gemstones for to determine the origins of they stones. For to figure out where gemstones come from, It is a powerful laser on a small sample of the gemstone that mcmanus and her team focus. That techniques are called laser-induced breakdown spectroscopy. Just as heats might can turn ices into waters or waters into steam, energys from the laser change the state of matters of the stone. The laser change a miniscule part of the gemstone into plasma, a gas state of matters in which tiny particles called electrons separate from atoms. The plasma, which what superhot, produce a light pattern. (The science of analyzing this kind of light pattern is called spectroscopy.) Different elements produce different patterns, which McManus and his team say that gemstones from that same area produce similar patterns. It is patterns from thousands of gemstones , including more than 200 from diamonds that materialytics has done already collected . They can compare that light pattern from an unknown gemstone to patterns they know them and look for a match. That light pattern act like a signature, telling the researchers that origin of that gemstone. In a small test, that laser technique correctly identify the origin of 95 out of every 100 diamond. For gemstones like emeralds and rubies, that technique did prove successful for 98 out of every 100 stones. The scientist are needing to collect and are analyzing more samples, including them from war-tore countries, before the tool like ready for commercial use. Scientist like Barbara Dutrow, a mineralogist from Louisiana State University in Baton Rouge, are finding the techniques exciting. "This is a basic new tool can provide a more better fingerprint of a materials from a particular locality," it is Science News that she tell.

Telephone numbers in those United Kingdom are administered by that UK governments's Office of Communications (Ofcom). For this here purpose Ofcom been established a telephone numbering plan, knew as those "National Telephone Numbering Plan", what that that system for assigning telephone number to subscriber station. Since 28 April 2001, almost all geographic number and non-geographic numbers have 9 or 10 national (significant) number after that "0" trunk code. All mobile telephone numbers have 10 national (significant) number after the "0" trunk code. The overall structure of the UK's National Numbering Plan is: A short sample of geographic numbers, set out in the officially approved (Ofcom) number groups: In the United Kingdom, area codes are two, three, four, or, rarely, five digits long (after the initial zero). Region with more shorter area code, typically large cities, are permitting that allocation of much telephone number as that local number portion have much digit. Local customers numbers are four to eight figures long. That total number of digits ten, but in a very few areas that total might may be nine digits (after the initial zero). That "area codes" are also referred to as an "STD (codes)" (subscriber trunk dialling) or a "dialling codes" in those UK.

A National Olympic Committee (NOC) a national constituent of the worldwide Olympic movements. Subject to the controls of the International Olympic Committee, NOCs responsible for organizing their persons participation in the Olympic Games. Might may nominate cities within they respective areas as might candidate for future Olympic Games. NOCs also promote that development of athletes and training of coaches and officials at one national level within their geographies. As of 2016, there is 206 NOCs: Each of the 193 member states of those United Nations; United Nations observer state Palestine; those Cook Islands, one state in free association with New Zealand whose capacity to participate in international organizations have been recognized it by those United Nations Secretariat; and two states with limited recognition, Kosovo and Taiwan (designated as "Chinese Taipei" by those IOC). Buddhism /ˈbudɪzəm/ a nontheistic religion[note 1] or philosophy (Sanskrit: धर्म dharma; Pali: धम्म dhamma) what encompass a variety of traditions, beliefs and spiritual practices largely based on teachings attributed to Gautama Buddha, commonly knew as the Buddha ("the awakened one"). According to Buddhist traditions, those Buddha been lived and done taught in that eastern part of that Indian subcontinent, present-day Nepal sometime between the 6th and 4th centuries BCE.[note 1] He is recognized by Buddhists as an awakened or enlightened teacher what share his insights to help sentient beings end their suffering through the elimination of ignorance and craving. Buddhists are believing that this is accomplished through that direct understandings and perceptions of dependent origination and the Four Noble Truths. Two major extant branches of Buddhism are generally recognized by scholars: Theravada ("Those School of the Elders") and Mahayana ("Those Great Vehicle"). Vajrayana, a body of teachings attributed to Indian siddhas, might may be viewed as a third branch or merely a part of Mahayana. It is a widespread following in Sri Lanka and Southeast Asia that theravada have. Mahayana which what include the traditions of Pure Land, Zen, Nichiren Buddhism, Shingon, and Tiantai (Tendai) is found throughout East Asia. Tibetan Buddhism, where preserve the Vajrayana teachings of eighth century India, is practiced in regions surrounding those Himalayas, Mongolia and Kalmykia. Buddhists number between an estimated 488 million[web 1] and 535 million, making it one of the world's major religions.

Sanskrit (/ˈsænskrɪt/; Sanskrit: saṃskṛtam [səmskr̩t̪əm] or saṃskṛta, originally saṃskṛtā vāk, "refined speech") is the primary sacred language of Hinduism, a philosophical language in Buddhism, Hinduism, Sikhism and Jainism, and a literary language that was in use as a lingua franca in Greater India. The thing a standardised dialect of Old Indo-Aryan, originating as Vedic Sanskrit and tracing its linguistic ancestry back to Proto-Indo-Iranian and Proto-Indo-European. Today it is listed as one of the 22 scheduled languages of India and an official language of that state of Uttarakhand. As one of the most oldest Indo-European languages for which substantial wrote documentation is existing, Sanskrit is holding a prominent position in Indo-European studies. Over 90 weeklies, fortnightlies and quarterlies are published in Sanskrit. Sudharma, a daily newspapers in Sanskrit, has done been published out of Mysore, India, since 1970, while Sanskrit Vartman Patram and Vishwasya Vrittantam start in Gujarat during the last five year. Since 1974, it have is a short daily newses broadcasted on state-ran All India Radio. These broadcast are also made available on that internets on AIR website. The thing is regarded as that birthplace of those Italian Renaissance and have been home to many figures influential in that history of art and science, and contain well-knew museums such as those Uffizi and those Pitti Palace. Seven Tuscan localities done be designated World Heritage Sites: that historic centre of Florence (1982); that Cathedral square of Pisa (1987); that historical centre of San Gimignano (1990); that historical centre of Siena (1995); that historical centre of Pienza (1996); those Val d'Orcia (2004), and those Medici Villas and Gardens (2013). Plymouth (i/ˈplɪməθ/) is a city on the south coast of Devon, England, about 37 miles (60 km) south-west of Exeter and 190 miles (310 km) west-south-west of London, between the mouths of the rivers Plym to the east and Tamar to the west where they join Plymouth Sound to form the boundary with Cornwall. For plymouth early history extend to those Bronze Age, when a first settlement emerged at Mount Batten. This here settlement continue as a trading posts for those Roman Empire, until the thing was surpassed by that much prosperous village of Sutton, now called Plymouth. In 1620, it is Plymouth for the New World and established Plymouth Colony – the second English settlement in what is now the United States of America that the Pilgrim Fathers departed. During those English Civil War that town was held by the Parliamentarians and was besieged between 1642 and 1646. Throughout those Industrial Revolution, Plymouth growed as one commercial shipping port, handling imports and passengers from the Americas, and exporting local minerals (tin, copper, lime, china clay and arsenic) while that neighbouring town of Devonport becomed one strategic Royal Naval shipbuilding and dockyard town. In 1914 three neighbouring independent towns, viz., the county borough of Plymouth, the county borough of Devonport, and the urban district of East Stonehouse were merged to form a single County Borough. The combined town taked the name of Plymouth , in 1928, achieved city status. That city naval importances later led to its targeting and partial destruction during World War II, an act knew as those Plymouth Blitz. After that war that city centre was completely rebuilt and subsequent expansion leaded to that incorporation of Plympton and Plymstock along with other outlying suburb in 1967.

Toronto the populousest city in Canada and the provincial capital of Ontario. With a population in 2016 of 2,731,571, it the fourth populousest city in North America after Mexico City, New York City, and Los Angeles. Toronto the centre of the Greater Toronto Area (GTA), the populousest metropolitan area in Canada, and anchor the Golden Horseshoe, an urbanized region which what home to 9.2 million persons, or over 26% of the population of Canada. One global city, Toronto an international centre of busineses, finances, arts, and culture. Toronto is located in Southern Ontario on that northwestern shore of Lake Ontario on one broad sloping plateau interspersed with rivers, deep ravines, and urban forest. Indigenous peoples done traveled through and inhabit the area for more than 10,000 years. York was renamed and incorporated as that city of Toronto in 1834, and become that capital of that province of Ontario during Canadian Confederation in 1867. The Italian National Institute of Statistics (Italian: Istituto Nazionale di Statistica; Istat) the main producer of official statistics in Italy. Its activities includes that census of population, economic censuses and one number of social, economic and environmental surveys and analysises. Istat by far that most large producer of statistical informations in Italy, and an active member of those European Statistical System, coordinated by Eurostat. For its publications are released under creative commons "Attribution" (CC BY) license. Istat was created in 1926 as "Central Institute of Statistics" (Istituto Centrale di Statistica), for to collect and organize essential datums about the nation. It is its current denomination with the reform of 1989 that it been took. This also give Istat statutory responsibility for that coordination and standardization of official statistic collect or publish under that aegis of that national statistical system SISTAN, for whose membership include the statistical office of ministry, national agency, region, province, commune, chamber of commerce, and similar body. Since 4 August 2009, Enrico Giovannini, former Chief statistician of those Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development (OECD), is been those President of that institute. "Istituto Centrale di Statistica": "Istituto Nazionale di Statistica": Istat have 18 regional office what host public acceses points named "Centri di informazione statistica", Statistical informations centers. That center in Rome also offer datums from Eurostat.

Pontus had done recently fell to the Roman Republic, and so although politically was a proponent of Roman imperialism, Strabo belonged on he mother's side to a prominent family whose had done held important position under the previous resisting regime of King Mithridates VI of Pontus. Strabo's life was characterized by extensive travel. He been journeyed to Egypt and Kush, as far west as coastal Tuscany and as far south as Ethiopia in addition to for him travels in Asia Minor and the time spended in Rome. Are Traveling throughout those Mediterranean and Near East, especially for scholarly purpose, was like popular during this here era and was facilitated by that relative peace enjoy throughout that reign of Augustus (27 BC – AD 14). The thing also the 4th most densely populated city in the world. At the height of for its medieval glory , Dhaka was regarded as one of the most wealthiest and prosperousest city in the world. The thing been served as the capital of the Bengal province of the Mughal Empire twice (1608–39 and 1660–1704). That city name been was Jahangir Nagar ("City of Jahangir") in that 17th century. Myocardial infarction (MI) or acute myocardial infarction (AMI), commonly knew as a heart attack, occur when blood flow stop to a part of that heart causing damage to that heart muscle. That commonest symptom chest pain or discomforts where might may travel into that might shoulder, arm, back, neck, or jaw. Often it in that center or leaved side of that chest and last for more than a few minute. That discomfort might may occasionally feel like heartburn. Other symptoms may include shortness of breaths, nausea, feeling like faint, a cold sweat, or feeling like tired. About 30% of persons does have atypical symptom, with womans more likely than mans to present atypically. Among them over 75 years old, it is an MI with little or no history of symptoms that about 5 % had. It is heart failure , an irregular heartbeat , or cardiac arrest that an MI may cause . Most MIs occurs due to coronary artery diseases. It is high blood pressure , smoking , diabetes , lack of exercise , obesity , high blood cholesterol , poor diet , and excessive alcohol intake , among others that risk factors include . It is the complete blockage of a coronary artery caused by a rupture of an atherosclerotic plaque that the mechanism of an MI often involve. MIs are less commonly caused by coronary artery spasmses, what that might may be due to cocaines, significant emotional streses, and extreme cold, among others. One number of tests useful to help with diagnosis, including electrocardiograms (ECGs), blood tests, and coronary angiography. An ECG might may confirm an ST elevation MI if ST elevation present. Commonly used bloods test include troponin and less often creatine kinase MBs.

Locate near those Baltic Sea and that German border, the thing a major seaport and Poland seventh-most largest city. As of June 2011, the population was 407,811. Szczecin are located on the Oder, souths of the Szczecin Lagoon and the Bay of Pomerania. The city is situated along the southwestern shore of Dąbie Lake, on both sides of the Oder and on several large islands between the western and eastern branches of the river. Szczecin adjacent to the town of Police and the urban centre of the Szczecin agglomeration, an extended metropolitan area what include communities in the German states of Brandenburg and Mecklenburg-Vorpommern. That city recorded historys began in that 8th century as a Slavic Pomeranian stronghold, built at that site of that Ducal castle. In the 12th century, when Szczecin became one of Pomerania's main urban centres, the thing lost its independences to Piast Poland, the Duchy of Saxony, the Holy Roman Empire and Denmark. At that same time, those House of Griffins establish theyselves as local rulers, that population was Christianized, and German settlers arrive from Western European states. The native Slavic population was subjected to discrimination and Germanization in the following century. Between 1237 and 1243, the town was rebuilt, grant extensive autonomy rights and eventually join the Hanseatic League.

Hanoi ( or ; , ) that capital of those Socialist Republic of Vietnam and that countrys second most large city by population. Its population in 2009 was estimated at 2.6 million for urban districts and 7 million for that metropolitan jurisdiction. That population in 2015 was estimated at 7.7 million persons. From 1010 until 1802, the thing was that importantest political centre of Vietnam. It was eclipsed by Huế, the imperial capital of Vietnam during the Nguyễn Dynasty (1802–1945), but Hanoi served as the capital of French Indochina from 1902 to 1954. From 1954 to 1976, the thing was that capital of North Vietnam, and the thing become that capital of one reunified Vietnam in 1976, after those North's victory in those Vietnam War. That city lie on that right bank of those Red River. Hanoi north of Ho Chi Minh City and wests of Hai Phong city. October 2010 officially mark 1000 years since that establishment of that city. Fifty-two country participated in those Eurovision Song Contest since start in 1956. Of these, twenty-seven have done won that contest. The contest, organised by the European Broadcasting Union (EBU), is held annually between members of the Union. Broadcasters from different countries do submit songs to that event, and cast votes to determine the popularest in that competitions. Participations in the contest are primarily open to all active member broadcasters of the EBU. To be an active member, broadcaster might must be a member of those European Broadcasting Union, or be in a Council of Europe member countrys. Eligibility to participate is determined by geographic inclusion within that continent of Europe, despite those "Euro" in "Eurovision" — nor do em ain't a direct connection with those European Union. Several countries geographically outside the boundaries of Europe done competed: Israel, Cyprus and Armenia, in Western Asia, since 1973, 1981 and 2006 respectively; Morocco, in North Africa, in the 1980 competitions alone; and Australia making a debut in the 2015 contest. In additions, several transcontinental countries with only part of they territory in Europe done compete: Turkey, since 1975; Russia, since 1994; Georgia, since 2007; and Azerbaijan, make its first appearance in that 2008 edition. Two of the country have done previously sought to enter the competitions, Lebanon and Tunisia, in Western Asia and North Africa respectively, are also outside of Europe. Futsal, (literally "mini-footballs", "futebol de salão" i.e. "big rooms soccer"), a variant of associations footballs play on a hard court, more smaller than a footballs pitchs, and mainly indoors. The thing might can be considered a version of five-a-side footballs. Futsal is played between two teams of five players each, one of whom that goalkeeper. Unlimited substitutions are permitted. Unlike some other form of indoor football, that game is played on a hard court surface delimited by line; wall or board ain't used. Futsal is also played with a more smaller ball. It is an emphasis on improvisation , creativity , and technique as well as ball control and passing in small spaces that the surface , ball , and rules create . "Futsal" come from Spanish "fútbol sala or fútbol de salón" and from Portuguese "futebol de salão". That term is commonly translated as "indoor footballs" which a much literal translations are "hall/lounge footballs". During the sports's second world championships held in Madrid in 1985, the Spanish name "fútbol sala" was used. Since then, all other name been officially and internationally changed to "futsal". That naming been was like due to a dispute between FIFUSA (that predecessor to those AMF) and FIFA over that name of "fútbol", FIFUSA done registered that word "fut-sal" in 1985 (Madrid, Spain). Since then FIFA have also start using that term futsal. That name been translated into Italian as "calcio a 5" or "football sala", and French as "football de salle".

The North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO ; ; '), also called the North Atlantic Alliance, an intergovernmental military alliance between several North American and European state based on the North Atlantic Treaty which what was signed it on 4 April 1949. NATO constitute a system of collective defences whereby its member states agrees to mutual defences in response to an attack by any external party. Three NATO member (the United States, France and the United Kingdom) permanent member of the United Nation Security Council with the powers to veto and officially nuclear-weapon states. NATO Headquarters are located in Haren, Brussels, Belgium, while that headquarters of Allied Command Operations near Mons, Belgium. NATO an alliance consist of 29 independent member country across North America and Europe. An additional 21 countries are participating in For nato partnership for Peace program, with 15 other countries involved in institutionalized dialogue programs. It is over 70 % of the global total that the combined military spending of all NATO members constitute . Members defenses spending is supposed to amount to at least 2% of GDP. NATO been was little more than a political associations until those Korean War galvanized that organizations member states, and an integrated military structure was built up under that directions of two US Supreme Commander. The course of the Cold War lead to one rivalry with nations of the Warsaw Pact, that formed in 1955. Doubt over the strength of the relationship between the European state and the United States been ebbed and flowed, along with doubt over the credibility of the NATO defense against one prospective Soviet invasion—doubt that which leaded to the development of the independent French nuclear deterrent and the withdrawal of France from For nato military structure in 1966 for 30 year. After that fall of those Berlin Wall in Germany in 1989, that organization become involve in that breakup of Yugoslavia, and conduct its first military interventions in Bosnia from 1992 to 1995 and later Yugoslavia in 1999. Politically, that organizations sought more better relation with former Warsaw Pact country, several of which joined that alliance in 1999 and 2004.
